# game: game:: code named from pretty-function strings

Owner: see `subsystems.tsv`. Source: the bulk naming pass of 2026-10-17.

## Map
- Every row here is L1. Its name comes from a `__PRETTY_FUNCTION__` or log string that the function references (`str:` anchor plus `xref:` sites); the full C++ name is in `notes` as `cxx=`.
- A function was named only when it references exactly one such name and no other function references that name. Names shared by several functions are kept as anchors only, since they usually mean an inlined callee.
- The string can still belong to a callee inlined into the named function. Read the decompile before raising a row to L2.
- Rows move to a feature lane (`session`, `fe_home`, ...) when someone takes them on; rename to `<Prefix>_<Verb>_<Noun>` at that point.
- neon:: names from the same pass are in `core` (prefix `Ne`, class `Ne` prefix dropped). The exe has no `ego::` strings.
- Hand-raised rows (not from the bulk pass): `g_Game_Root` (0x14601db08), the root object whose +0xa0 context holds the UI module at +0x28 (see core.md, fullscreen video), and `g_Game_FlowLinkStay` (0x146cb2280), the link a flow state's onUpdate writes to stay put; `g_Career_FlowLinkContinue` follows it at 0x146cb2284.

## Save containers
<a id="save-containers"></a>
Raised by keepsaves (2026-10-27, static, Steam d873a1c8) for the lost Progressive careers.
- The profile save (`savegame@profile#NXDSMWW.BWW`) is one tree of save-data nodes (`structs/SaveDataContainer.h`, base ctor `Ne_Construct_SaveDataContainer` 0x14364d570). Its root `save_game` is `SaveDataSystem+0x20` (`Game_SaveDataSystem_Construct` 0x143654650, `vt_Game_SaveDataRootContainer`).
- Every `game_modes` row with `register_save_container` true puts its mode container, named by `save_data.name`, directly under the root (`Game_GameModes_BuildMode` 0x1407cae80, attach at 0x1407cf910). The mode's `SaveSlotGameModeComponent` (`Game_SaveSlotManager_Construct` 0x1429cd140, built in `Game_ModeChampionship_Build` at 0x1407d5b62) holds every career slot of that mode with its raw data blob (`Game_SaveSlotManager_Deserialise` 0x1429d6810, `Game_SaveSlotManager_LoadSlot` 0x1429d42f0). Slots are never validated against the database at load (`Game_SaveSlotManager_ReadSlotInfo` 0x1429d4a30 only copies fields).
- Load: `Game_SaveData_DeserialiseContainer` (0x14364f3a0, entered for the root from .data1 at 0x150b20d82) matches each child bag of `containers` to a registered child by FNV-1a64 of the name. A child bag nobody registered is dropped, unless the node's `retain_unknown` bool (+0x100/+0x108) is true: then the bag is copied into the node's retained map (+0x190, count +0x1c8). The root is built with `retain_unknown` false.
- Save: the bag-path `Game_SaveData_SerialiseContainer` (0x14364e000) writes the registered children and, with `retain_unknown` true, every retained bag back under its own name. The profile save does not take that path: the .data1 save at 0x150b16391 calls `Game_SaveData_SerialiseTimed` with no bag and a stream, so the root goes through the inline twin `Game_SaveData_SerialiseContainer_2` (0x143651030), which writes only the registered children and never reads `retain_unknown` or the retained map.
- So a career content whose mode row has its own `save_data.name` (Progressive: `driver_career_progressive`, statId 82; F1 1990: `driver_career_1990`) loses every career the first time the profile is saved in a session that lacks the addon: the mode container is not registered, the root drops its bag at load, and the next profile save writes the root without it.
- Names resolve: the profile is written with the inline encoding (key strings in the stream), and the stream reader 0x143934620 (called by `Game_CompressedSave_OnReadResult` 0x14364bb50) adds every key with the bag add-by-name 0x143932a40, which registers the name in the string table 0x146129740 that the retain path looks the bag hash up in (0x14392a8b0).
- Turning `retain_unknown` on for the root does not fix the loss and crashes the game (T009 run 2, 2026-10-27): the retain path deep-copies the unknown bag (0x143931350) into the 3 MB `SAVE_DATA` heap while the decoded profile bag still lives there, and the copy of `driver_career_progressive` failed with Ne heap error 0xc (requested 16,368, free 5,200). Even with room, the inline profile save would not write the retained bag. A keeper has to hold the bytes outside the game heaps and splice them into the inline stream: the stream codec is vtable 0x14568e060 (+0x8 bag writer 0x1439340c0, +0x10 bag reader 0x143934620, +0x18 header value 1), and the root's `containers` block in 0x143651030 is a child count followed by name, type id and body per child.

### Keeping unclaimed containers by bytes
<a id="save-splice"></a>
Raised by splicesaves (2026-10-27, static). The bytes of an unclaimed root child are kept outside the game heaps and written back into the inline profile save.
- Load: `site_Game_SaveData_UnclaimedChild` (0x14364fef0) is the no-match join of the child loop in `Game_SaveData_DeserialiseContainer`; rdi = the container, `*[rsp+0x88]` = the property of the current child bag. For the root (`vt_Game_SaveDataRootContainer`) the property's key hash (`NePropertyTypeVtbl.key_offset`) gives the name through `Ne_StringTable_Lookup`, and `NePropertyVtbl.value` gives the bag. `Game_SaveData_WriteBag` writes it through a `NeBagCodec` twice, as `0x1429ce380` does for a career slot: into a `NeCountingStream` for the size, then into a `NeMemWriteStream` over a buffer the core owns. Only bag-typed children are kept (type id against `k_Ne_PropertyType_Bag`).
- Format: the root's `containers` block in `Game_SaveData_SerialiseContainer_2` is the key, the bag type id (u32), the codec header (8 bytes, 1), the child count (8 bytes), then per child its name (stream vt+0x50 length byte, vt+0x48 chars), the bag type id `[rsp+0x58]` (u32) and its body. The reader `0x143934620` reads every child body as a bag, and `Game_SaveData_WriteBag` is its inverse, so name + type id + the captured `WriteBag` bytes is an entry the next load reads back as the same bag.
- Save: `site_Game_SaveData_ContainersCount` (0x143651bbf) adds the kept count to `[rsp+0x90]` before the count is written; `site_Game_SaveData_ContainersChildren` (0x143651be4) writes the kept entries to `[rsp+0x50]` right after it. A failed write stores the code in `[r12]` and continues at `site_Game_SaveData_ContainersDone` (0x143651cbc), the game's own path for a failed write in that block.

### Where a save decodes, and the career slot id
<a id="save-heaps"></a>
Raised by w4r (2026-10-28, static, Steam d873a1c8) for the career snapshots' `swofty_registry` entry.
- Load: `Game_CompressedSave_OnReadResult` takes the largest free block (heap vt+0x80(heap, 1), the "Largest" line of `Ne_ReportMemoryError`) of SAVE_TEMP_ALLOC_HUGE, or of SAVE_TEMP_ALLOC when the serialiser config dword `[[serialiser+8]+0]` is non-zero (`cmovne` at 0x14364c30a), and makes it a pool named SAVE_PROPERTY_BAG_TEMP (`Ne_Init_TempPool_Thunk`). The root bag `{pool, 0, 0}` is decoded by `Ne_BagCodec_ReadBag`; every child bag gets its parent's allocator and every leaf value is constructed with the bag's allocator (`Ne_Bag_ReadValue`, type vt+0x50), so the whole decoded profile, unclaimed root children included, lives in that pool. After the root deserialise (0x14364c6f0) `Ne_Bag_Clear` and `Game_CompressedSave_FreeTempPool_Thunk` free it all; the latter logs `PropertyBagTempPool size= sizeUsed= peak=` on channel CompressedSaveDataSerialiser.
- Heap sizes (game.dat `system/memory_map.xml`, read with `packages/formats` nefs): SAVE_TEMP 50M, SAVE_TEMP_ALLOC 75M and SAVE_TEMP_ALLOC_HUGE 200M ("Property Bag Alloc Only Pool"), SAVE_DATA 3M ("Persistant save data pool for save data components and save data for removed DLC").
- So an unclaimed root child never touches SAVE_DATA on the way in: the T009 crash (free 5,200 of 3,145,728 in SAVE_DATA) was the retain deep copy 0x143931350 of a 3.5 MB bag into the container's SAVE_DATA allocator, not the decode. The splice copies the child's bytes into core memory before the pool is freed.
- Save: `Game_CompressedSave_AddWriteRequest` builds the same pool from SAVE_TEMP_ALLOC's largest free block and writes the profile through a compressing stream (0x14568e0a0) into a memory write stream of max(0xe00000, [cfg+0xa8]) bytes allocated from serialiser+0x248 (a write that does not fit returns 0x37). The four save kinds register 10, 14, 12 and 25 MB (0x14365a8b0). The splice writes the kept bytes straight into that stream.
- Temp pool (`vtable 0x14568df40`, savepool 2026-11-01, static): `Ne_TempPool_Alloc` is a bump allocator over `[pool+0x98, pool+0x98+[pool+0xa0])` with the cursor at `+0xa8`; nothing is ever freed, so a growing vector (`0x14393a770`, a ushort-counted array of 8-byte property pointers that doubles its capacity) leaves every old array behind. The write pool is the whole largest free block of SAVE_TEMP_ALLOC (75M, `heap_internal`, virtual, `virtualExpandPercent` 0, created by `Ne_MemoryMap_CreateHeapInternal`), so the profile's property bags (everything the game serialises itself, not the kept containers) must fit in 75M, against 200M for a read. Past the end `Ne_TempPool_ReportError` calls `Ne_ReportMemoryError` fatal (pool+0x80 bit 0), which ends in `Ne_FatalAbort` (a deliberate `mov [1], 0xdeaddead`: the access violation at `Ne_FatalAbort+0x4` is the abort, not a fault in `ReportError`). Yukki (1.7.7, 2026-11-01) died this way at a 656,560 byte request, the 41,035 to 82,070 entry growth of one such array, 0.1 s after the splice wrote back 4.3 MB of kept bytes; the kept bytes go to the memory write stream (serialiser+0x248 allocator), not to the pool. Core raises SAVE_TEMP_ALLOC to 512M (200M was too small for a profile with ~18 managers, max._verstappen1 1.7.17: 199.5 of 200 MiB used) at `site_Ne_MemoryMapInternalHeapSize` (0x143972474, r15 = size).
- Career slot id: `SaveSlotInfo` (structs/SaveSlotInfo.h) starts with `slot_id` and `slot_guid`, both uint64 info keys copied verbatim at load (`Game_SaveSlotManager_ReadSlotInfo`) and written back under the same keys (0x1429d2bc0). `Game_SaveSlotManager_SelectSlot` loads a career by `slot_id` and sets `SaveSlotManager.current`; `site_Game_SaveSlot_Selected` sees the slot (rbx) before its blob deserialises. `Game_InstantRace_FlagSaveSlot` gets the slot as rdx before a save. Slots are per mode container, so a career is named by the mode's `save_data.name` plus `slot_guid` (or `slot_id`). Where a new slot's `slot_guid` is made is not found (L0: neither the slot list code nor the info writer generate it).

## Career participants {#career-participants}

re-career-teams, 2026-10-28 (static; tests T133, T134, T135). A team key is a u32: kind byte (0 a database team, 1/2 custom or My Team teams) | team stat id << 16.

- **The championship's participants** (`Game_Championship_HasTeam` 0x140aea5f0, body `Game_Championship_HasTeam_Body` 0x149423b70, L2) are not stored: the check walks the championship's game setup modes (+0x318..+0x320), their entrant setup modes (+0x10[], count +0x70, flag +0x24c + kind*4 == 1) and those modes' entrant lists (+0x20[], count +0x120, 0x88 rows, team kind +0x58, stat +0x5a) or selection domain (+0x18 -> +0x60..+0x68). So the participants are the teams of the parsed database lists the setup modes name.
- **The career's participants** are its entrant rows. `Game_CareerSetup_ResetEntrants` 0x140c34960 and `Game_CareerSetup_BuildEntrants` 0x140c2a870 (front-end callers only) copy the data group's entrant list (entrants registry +0x560, index from setup vt +0x268) into CareerEntity +0x2d8 and remember it at +0xe18. The registry's team check `Game_CareerRegistry_HasTeam` 0x1407c4080 (slot +0x1c0) answers from those rows unless CareerEntity +0x10c8 is set, when it defers to the championship. `Game_CareerSetup_ValidateEntrantTeams_Thunk` 0x140c301e0 clears team and car of every listed non-player row the check refuses. `Game_CareerRegistry_ListEntrants_Thunk` 0x1407c41e0 (slot +0x1c8) lists the rows; contracts are rebuilt from it.
- **No re-copy at a season start** was found: the stock game keeps one team set per career and changes rows one at a time (`Career_Set_EntrantData_Thunk`). T134 checks it.
- **Per-team R&D models come from the save by team key** on load (`Game_UpgradesComponent_Deserialise` 0x14123f930 -> `Career_Add_TeamUpgrades`). The creation path into `Career_Add_TeamUpgrades` was not found (no other direct caller).
- Design that uses this: work/std16/RE-CAREER-TEAMS.md (core `career::apply_teams`).

- 2026-10-28 09:05 lapguard: 2026-10-28 lapguard: ERS new-lap crash (1.5.21, F1_25.exe+0x25180f7, Steam and EA 0x142511477). Game_Progress_ClampToSingleLap (0x1446034b0) is the whole per-vehicle progress update, not just the clamp; its vehicle_newlap post (0x144603a7e) runs Game_ErsSystem_HandleVehicleNewLap synchronously, which reads entry+0x28 of every ERS participant before the matching one without the null check Game_ErsSystem_Update has. Game_ErsSystem_BindVehicles writes 0 there for an entity without a vehicle component. Core skips those entries (native/src/core/ers_newlap.cpp, site_Game_ErsNewLap_VehicleLookup to site_Game_ErsNewLap_NextVehicle); T011 asks for the in-game check. L0, open: why the first vehicle_newlap comes 0.1-0.5 s after StateLoadSession at a community track in Time Trial and stock tracks do not crash. The programme (vt_Game_PracticeTrackAcclimatisation 0x1457b40c0, class name PracticeTrackAcclimatisation) keeps its targets in self+0x350..+0x358 (0x370-byte corner entries); Game_PracticeTrackAcclimatisation_CopyTargets is their only writer and copies them from g_Game_TrackAcclimatisationResource (0x146076158), the TrackAcclimatisationResource that Game_TrackAcclimatisationResource_Create parses from a resource file (bag node CornerData, then the node named by practice_programmes.practice_programme_folder.folder_name; Corner/Apex gates with world positions, offsets, speeds and hit radii). The DB rows the launcher clones for an imported track (practice programme rows, track_acclimatisation.* settings) are not the source of the targets, so the gap is the track's resource data: an imported track has no CornerData for its own layout, the vector stays null, Game_PracticeTrackAcclimatisation_Start returns on the empty vector (the programme never starts, +0x38 stays 0), but Game_PracticeProgramme_HandleDriveOut calls slot +0xf0 for every programme and Game_PracticeTrackAcclimatisation_RefreshState indexes entry [0]. Not a small launcher data fix: copying a donor track's TrackAcclimatisationResource would put the donor's gate positions on another layout. Core returns from the refresh on an empty vector (native/src/core/track_acclim.cpp); every other reader of +0x350 in the class either checks begin != end or sits behind +0x38/+0x39, which only a non-empty Start sets. T012 asks for the in-game check. L0, open: whether the resource is absent (global null) or present with an empty CornerData node at an imported track; who writes 0x146076158 (no .xtls writer).

- 2026-10-30 21:59 icons-off: Platform icon gates (site_Game_PlatformIconGate_*): they gate only the rows' show/visible bool; the same rows resolve the tag's icon texture before the gate (e.g. Team_Update_TimingPill 0x14175dc90 stores it into the pill's platform_icon node whatever the MP mode), and the HUD timing tower, the lobby grid list and the results still showed the Steam icon in LAN rooms with every gate hiding. - 2026-10-30 20:41 rules: rules 2026-10-31: how a pit stop's time maps onto the crew animation (static, decomps 1403bc300, 1434d0cb0). 0x1434d0cb0 (thunk, .data1 body 0x150457f40) returns nominal / target: nominal = mean of two pit lane config floats (pit lane +0x58 object, +0x8/+0xc, +0x10/+0x14, +0x18/.., +0x20/+0x24 by the front-wing and 4th flags) plus per-mistake additions (+0x11c/+0x120), written to *out; target = 0x141036ba0 on the PitCrewTraining component (+0x2f8), the training band / team pit times. Game_PitStopActionObserver_SetTransitionSequence 0x1403bc300 then takes target = nominal / ratio, picks the sequence family 258 (ratio < 1) or 21, sets the playback rate base / target (base 2.58f or 2.1f) and stores it in the pit lane car record *(pitlane+0x68+car*0x10)+0 (pitStopCrewSpeed; +4 is the pit status), resetting PitStopInstance+0x33c and [+0x1f0]+0x3bc to 1.0 first. Suffixes FWA (front wing adjust), 5SP/10SP (penalty, when 0x142f29600 and g 0x145f9677c is 5 or 10), and per-team text at +0x368. Game_PitStopInstance_PlaySequence 0x1403b36a0 instantiates it into +0x3b0. The stationary time is therefore one uniform rate over a pre-authored sequence (pitstop_26.erp, 223 BXMLX sequences, 30 fps: StartJobDoStandardStop21 events Start 15, JC 77, Stop 78, SC 108; tyre_gun_off ~20-26, tyre_gun_on ~61, jack_move 73). Per-phase timing needs re-authored sequences (AnimKey attrs: time_f, duration_f, end_time_f, animStartTime_f, blendInTime, clamped, loop, updateAtEnd) or a per-frame rate hook on the Ne sequence instance, whose time/rate fields are not in the KB.
